NUM	<p>d73d_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795800;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #795800;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795800;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #156b6b;">Gene K</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #156b6b;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #156b6b;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene T</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #7142a8;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>cd8f_e890</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #496a09;">Gene M</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #496a09;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #496a09;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene W</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #933866;">Gene X</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #933866;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>5c02_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #894d00;">Gene F</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #894d00;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #196f36;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #88388c;">Gene P</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #88388c;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>8918_2860</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #66600c;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #66600c;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #66600c;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #1e658c;">Gene K</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #1e658c;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e658c;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #933866;">Gene M</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #933866;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #933866;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>6735_cd3c</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944220;">Gene F</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #944220;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #944220;">Gene F</span> is covered in a dense layer of hairs, giving it a soft appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene N</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene X</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #7142a8;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; NUM	<p>dc08_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene A</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #9c363f;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #66600c;">Gene B</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #66600c;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #66600c;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene M</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #196f36;">Gene A</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #196f36;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>e439_bbfa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene E</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #9c363f;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #66600c;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #66600c;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #66600c;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene Y</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>6ef0_bf92</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #66600c;">Gene B</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #66600c;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #66600c;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene C</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene T</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #7142a8;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>fe0f_bbfa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #196f36;">Gene D</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #196f36;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>f145_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944220;">Gene A</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #944220;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #196f36;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #315da0;">Gene W</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #315da0;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>9420_bbfa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795800;">Gene C</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #795800;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #795800;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006e57;">Gene K</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006e57;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e57;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene N</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #7142a8;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>8c92_ecf8</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #496a09;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #496a09;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #1e658c;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #88388c;">Gene Y</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #88388c;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>daaa_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944220;">Gene E</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #944220;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>52c8_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #894d00;">Gene K</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #894d00;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #894d00;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697d;">Gene N</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #00697d;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697d;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #7142a8;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7142a8;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #795800;">Gene H</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #795800;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>12e8_2860</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #006e57;">Gene A</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006e57;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006e57;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #315da0;">Gene H</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #315da0;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #315da0;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #933866;">Gene M</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #933866;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #933866;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944220;">Gene A</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #944220;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene B</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #9c363f;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9c363f;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #196f36;">Gene J</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #196f36;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #196f36;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #4c4bc4;">Gene T</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #4c4bc4;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #4c4bc4;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; color: #000000;">6,200</span></td></tr></table><p><ul> <li>The distance between genes B and J is 25 cM</li><li>The distance between genes B and T is 39 cM</li><li>The distance between genes J and T is 16 cM</li><li>The correct gene order determined from these distances is <strong>BJT</strong></li></ul></p> <h6>Step-by-Step Instructions for Calculating Interference</h6><ul> <li>Step 1: Count the observed number of double crossovers from the data table.</li> <li>Step 2: Calculate the probability of independent crossovers between distant genes.</li> <ul><li>Multiply the two individual crossover probabilities (based on their distance) for both adjacent gene pairs.</li></ul> <li>Step 3: Determine the expected number of double crossovers.</li> <ul><li>Multiply the combined probability (from Step 2) by the total progeny count.</li></ul> <li>Step 4: Calculate the Coefficient of Coincidence (CoC).</li> <ul><li>Divide the observed number of double crossovers (from Step 1) by the expected number (from Step 3).</li></ul> <li>Step 5: Calculate Interference.</li> <ul><li>Interference is given by the formula: Interference = 1 - CoC.</li></ul></ul><p>In genetic studies, interference refers to the phenomenon where the occurrence of a crossover in one region of a chromosome reduces the likelihood of another crossover occurring nearby, thereby affecting the expected genetic ratios.</p> <h6>Question</h6> <p>Based on the traits expressed in the offspring, <strong>calculate the percentage of interference</strong> between genes B and T.</p> <h6>Important Answer Guidelines</h6><p><ul><li><i>Important Tip 1:</i> Your calculated distance between the pair of genes should be a whole number. Finding a decimal in your answer, such as 5.5, indicates a mistake was made. Please provide your answer as a complete number without fractions or decimals.</li><li><i>Important Tip 2:</i> Your answer should be written as a numerical value only, with no spaces, commas, or units such as "cM" or "map units". For example, if the distance is fifty one centimorgans, simply write "51".</li></ul></p>	75.00000000	0.10000000
